Let's focus on implementing the LP algorithm robustly and efficiently. The state-of-art description: Starting from normalized f (sum sqrt(2n)?), compute M=||f*f||∞. Solve LP maximizing sum b (they call g) subject to b≥0 and ||f*g||∞≤M. Rescale g to match normalization, update f=(1-t)f+t g. Repeat.

Pseudo:
def game_refine(a, budget_s, seed=0, verbose=False):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    n = len(a)
    a = np.asarray(a,dtype=float); n=a.size
    a = np.clip(a, 1e-15, None)
    a *= n / a.sum()
    log_a = np.log(a)
    best_a = a.copy(); best_obj = objective_fft(a)
    FA = rfft(a, N)
    # Initialize g as a (or best start)
    log_g = log_a.copy()
    g_log = log_a.copy()
    no_improve = 0
    tau = n*0.2
    tau_min = n*1e-4? Maybe for n=4096 tau_min=0.4? b values ~n, tau in same units. If tau too small, p binary. Let's set tau = max(1.0, n*0.1) initial, decay factor 0.98 each outer, min max(0.1, n*1e-4) (for n=4096 min0.4). But b values differences O(1?), max~n, small differences maybe. Use tau = max(1.0, 0.01*n) maybe 40. For n=4096, 1% n=40, difference small? Maybe okay. Start 0.2n=800, decay to 0.01n=40.
    eta_inner = 0.5
    cap_rate = 2.0
    for outer in range(max_iters until time):
       # inner minimax g
       for inner in range(inner_iters e.g. 8):
           G = rfft(g,N); c=irfft(FA*G,N)[:2n-1].real; c=np.clip(c,0)
           mc = c.max()
           # Maybe stop inner if mc <= best_cross? We don't know.
           p = softmax(c, tau)
           P = rfft(p,N); d=irfft(FA*conj(P),N).real
           d = extract; d=np.clip(d,0)
           # update log_g: use inc = eta_inner*(d.max()-d), cap
           inc = np.minimum(eta_inner*(d.max()-d), cap_rate)
           update log_g += inc; normalize g; log_g consistent
           # maybe also symmetrize? no.
       # evaluate g
       og = objective_fft(g)
       # candidates: g, blends with a
       cand_list: g, (1-lam)a+lam*g for lam in [0.5,0.7,0.9,1.0]
       choose cand_best with lowest obj; maybe also current a.
       if cand_best is a and no improvement, try random small blend? 
       # But if we set a to cand_best, outer loop may stagnate if best not improving. We can still update a with best blend even if objective slightly higher? Maybe not.
       if best_cand_obj < best_obj*(1-1e-6): best update
       # set a = best_cand (or a + small*g) for next outer. If best_cand is a, maybe set a = (1-smallsteps)a + smallstep*g (smallstep 0.1) to move.
       a = best_cand; update log_a, FA.
       # decay tau, inner iterations
       # occasional random restart/perturb if no_improve>...: a = current * exp(noise), renormalize.
    return best_a,best_obj

Let's implement a general exponential update function:
def exp_update(log_a, tau, eta, cap, centered=False):
   a = exp_from_log(log_a,n)
   b=conv(a); p=softmax(b,tau); d=crosscorr(p,a)
   if centered:
       target = np.dot(a,d)/n  # weighted mean
       inc = eta*(target - d)
   else:
       inc = eta*(d.max()-d)
   inc = np.clip(inc, -cap, cap) # if centered can negative for low d? Wait target-d positive if d below target; max-d nonnegative. For minimize, inc positive for low d, negative for high d. If we use max-d, inc nonnegative only, no negative, but since max gets 0, relative lower. It doesn't reduce high by negative, just doesn't increase them; after normalization, high decrease relative. That's fine and stable. For centered, some below get positive, above get negative. Could overflow; cap both.
   update log_a += inc; normalize.
Return a,new_obj,d maybe.
For direct, use max-d to avoid decreasing low? Actually all get nonnegative, max zero -> relative decrease of max. Good.
For inner g, similarly max-d.

Cap: max relative log increase per iter = log_ratio, e.g., 2 (ratio 7). If eta*(max-d) > log_ratio, cap. Since max-d nonnegative. log_a += capped. Then high d not decreased beyond normalization. Good.
This means each coordinate's log can only increase by up to log_ratio per iter; high ones not incremented, so relative ratio up to e^{log_ratio}. Over many iters, can create large disparities. Use log_ratio maybe 0.5? e^0.5=1.65 per iter; many iters strong. maybe 1.0 (2.7). Let's use 1.0 for early, decay? We can tune.
Eta scaling: d range maybe up to few. Setting inc = min(eta*(max-d), log_ratio). If eta=1 and range 1, cap1. Good. If range 10, limited cap. Use eta=1.

Temperature tau: p=softmax(b,tau). If tau large, p smooth, d range small, updates slow; if small, p hard, range large but cap. We can anneal tau. Start tau=0.5*n? maybe. Decay per iteration by 0.98 to min n*1e-4.

Direct update line search? We can just use cap and eta. To improve, after each iter maybe if no objective improvement for X, increase tau (anneal) or reset.
But we need efficient evaluation. Each update requires conv and crosscorr. direct 1 update =2 FFT forward? Need b and d: A=fft(a), B=ifft(A^2), P=fft(p), D=ifft(A*conj(P)). Could compute A once per update. 2 forward (A,P) +2 inverse. For n=4096 N=8192, okay. inner uses same but A fixed outer.

Game_refine more concrete:
def game_refine(a, budget_s, seed=0, verbose=False, max_iters=None):
    n=len(a); N=next_pow2(2*n-1)
    a = positive_normalize(a,n)
    log_a = np.log(a)
    best_a=a.copy(); best_obj=objective_fft_from_normalized? compute b.
    FA = np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    # setup g maybe from a, maybe random
    g = a.copy(); log_g=np.log(g)
    tau = n*0.2; tau_min = max(0.5, n*1e-4); decay=0.97
    eta = 1.0; cap=1.0
    inner_iters_base=8
    no_improve=0
    last_check=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       # maybe adjust tau, inner
       for _ in range(inner_iters):
          # update g using current FA
          G = np.fft.rfft(g,N); c=np.fft.irfft(FA*G,N, n=N); c=c[:2*n-1]; c=np.clip(c,0); mc=c.max()
          p=softmax(c, tau)
          d=cross_from_fa(FA,p)
          inc = np.minimum(eta*(d.max()-d), cap)
          log_g += inc
          g,log_g=exp_from_log(log_g,n)
       # evaluate g
          # candidates
          cands = [g]
          objs = [objective_fft(g)]
          for lam in (0.5,0.75,1.0): cands.append((1-lam)*a + lam*g); objs.append(objective_fft(c))
          # also a
          objs.insert(0, best current obj?); cands.insert(0,a)
          choose min obj as next_a (if min is a and no improvement? choose a with largest lam? Maybe choose g if og<aa_obj else blend 0.5 to progress)
          if next_obj < best_obj*(1-1e-7): best update
          if next_obj < best_obj? no.
          a=next_a; log_a=np.log(a); FA=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
          tau*=decay; if tau<tau_min: tau=tau_min
          # inner_iters maybe increase over time? use int(base + 4*elapsed/total)
       # random perturb every e.g. 100 outer if no_improve>20:
          if no_improve > 30:
             noise = exp(rng.normal(0,0.1,n)); a=a*noise; normalize; update; no_improve=0
    return best_a,best_obj

   if obj<best: best update
   tau decay; periodically perturb.

Game refinement in detail:
Given a, we want find g that minimizes max cross a*g. The inner sequence g can be updated using multiplicative weights. After inner, candidate blends. If inner g has self objective even lower, use it. This is similar to finding a better point in the same cross-bound set.
We can also use the LP solution g directly as new a (lam=1) if it improves; SoA uses small t to limit g*g, but line search can choose.
Inner update for g:
- c = a*g
- p = softmax(c, tau)
- d_i = ∑ p_k a_{k-i}
- inc = min(eta*(d.max()-d), cap)
- g *= exp(inc), normalize.
This decreases g at coordinates with high cross with active lags. It finds a sequence g whose mass avoids active high cross lags. Good.
Number inner iters: Since g starts as a or previous g. We can reuse previous g across outer (warm start). For new a, g might need converge. Use 10-50 depending time.
Temperature tau for c: c max maybe n, c average n/2. Use tau = T*n. Start T=0.1, decay to 0.005. If tau small, p top; if too small, inner oscillates among lags. Use moderate.
Eta_inner: d range up to maybe max(a) ~10. cap log ratio. Use eta=1, cap=1.0. Maybe if a smooth d range <1, no cap, small update. Increase eta if changes small? Outer blend: We can choose lam=1 if g objective better, else lam=0.5. To ensure monotonic in cross? We can also choose lam by minimizing objective among [g,0.7,0.5,0.3,0.1]. Let's refine direct MMW with detailed schedules to maximize chance.
We can write a function `run_exponential_sequence(n, budget, init, mode)` that uses either direct or game. Let's decide final: Use game with inner minimax, plus periodic direct. It is aligned with SOTA.
Pseudo for `run_exponential_sequence`:
```
def run_exponential_sequence(n, budget_s, init=None, seed=0, verbose=True):
    t_start=time.monotonic()
    deadline=t_start+budget_s-2
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    if init is None: init=uniform
    a=normalize(init,n)
    log_a=np.log(a)
    best_a=a.copy(); best_obj=objective_fft(a)
    N=next_pow2(2*n-1)
    FA=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    g=a.copy(); log_g=np.log(g)
    tau = n*0.1
    tau_min = max(0.5, n*1e-4)
    eta=1.0; cap=1.0
    inner_iters=10
    no_imp=0
    direct_count=0
    iter=0
    while time < deadline:
       # direct step every 5 cycles?
       if iter % 5 == 0:
          # direct update a with p from b
          b=self_conv_from_fa(FA)
          p=softmax(b,tau)
          d=cross(FA,p)
          inc=np.minimum(eta*(d.max()-d), cap)
          log_a += inc
          a,log_a=normalize_log(log_a,n)
          FA=fft(a,N)
       # inner
       for _ in range(inner_iters):
          G=fft(g,N)
          c=irfft(FA*G,N)[:2n-1]; clip
          p=softmax(c,tau)
          d=cross(FA,p)
          inc=np.minimum(eta*(d.max()-d), cap)
          log_g += inc
          g,log_g=normalize_log(log_g,n)
       # candidate blend
       # compute objective of g: G=fft(g,N) (already? after last inner G from g? We can compute og=2*max(irfft(G*G))/n)
       og=objective_from_ff(G,G?) # need max self
       aa_obj=2*max(b)/n? b from FA maybe current; but after direct b updated.
       # choose a_new
       if og < aa_obj:
           # try lam=1,0.7,0.5? evaluate maybe two
           cand1=(1-0.5)*a+0.5*g; obj1=objective_fft(cand1)
           if og < obj1: a_new=g
           else: a_new=cand1
       else:
           a_new=0.9*a+0.1*g  # small move
       # optional line search lam best among [a_new, 0.5,1] using objective
       # update best
       fail? 
       a,log_a=normalize(a_new,n); FA=fft(a,N)
       # periodically keep g warmed: if new a very different, maybe g=0.9g+0.1a? already g maintained.
       # adaptive tau
       tau*=0.985; if tau<tau_min: tau=tau_min
       # inner_iters maybe: 5 + int(15*progress)
       # if no improvement for 50: random perturb a
       if no_imp>50:
           a *= exp(rng.normal(0,0.3,n)); normalize; log_a=...
           no_imp=0
       iter+=1
    # final maybe direct refinement with last time
    return best_a, best_obj
```

def lp_refine_seq(a, budget_s, max_lp_n=1024, verbose=True):
   # a normalized sum=1 for LP, but objective uses sum1.
   n=len(a); a=a/(a.sum())
   best=a.copy(); best_obj=objective_seq_sum1(a)
   deadline=...
   consec_fail=0
   while time <= deadline and n>=32:
      t0=time.monotonic()
      T = np.zeros((2*n-1,n), float)
      # fill columns vectorized:
      for i in range(n): T[i:i+n, i] = a
      A_ub = np.hstack((T, -np.ones((2*n-1,1))))
      c = np.zeros(n+1); c[-1]=1
      A_eq = np.array([[np.ones(n), 0.0]])
      b_eq = np.array([1.0])
      bounds=[(0,None)]*n + [(0,None)]
      res=linprog(c, A_ub, np.zeros(2*n-1), A_eq, b_eq, bounds=bounds, method='highs', timeout=5)
      if res.success:
          g=res.x[:n]; g=np.maximum(g,0); s=g.sum(); if s>0: g/=s
          # evaluate candidates
          og=objective_seq_sum1(g)
          cands=[g]
          for lam in [0.5,0.8]: cands.append((1-lam)*a+lam*g)
          choose best cand obj as a_next
          if best_cand_obj < best_obj*(1-1e-6): best=...
          a=a_next
          consec_fail=0
      else: consec_fail++
      # if time per LP too high, break
      if consec_fail>3: break
   return best
```

def _self_conv(a):
 n=len(a); N=1; while N<2*n-1: N<<=1; fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N); b=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N); return b[:2*n-1].real, N, fa
```

```
def _objective(a):
 n=len(a); S=a.sum(); if S<=1e-15 return inf
 b,_,_= _self_conv(a); return float(2*n*b.max()/(S*S))
```

def _lp_refine(init,n,budget_s,seed=0,timeout_lp=5,verbose=True):
   t0; deadline=t0+budget_s-2
   if not HAVE_SCIPY: return _run_exponential...

   # LP digits: use sum=1 for LP but keep objective sum n? We can normalize a LPA=a/n? Let's maintain a_sum1 = a/n for LP. Objective invariant if scale. Use a1=a/n (sum1).
   best=a.copy(); best_obj=_objective(a)
   fail=0
   while time<deadline:
      a1=a/n
      # if n large >1024, maybe reduce? We'll call with appropriate n.
      T = toeplitz(np.concatenate((a1, np.zeros(n-1))), np.concatenate((a1[:1], np.zeros(n-1))))? Exponential refine for larger n:
```
def _exponential_refine(init,n,budget_s,seed=0,mode='game',verbose=True):
    t0; deadline=t0+budget_s-2
    a=_normalize_seq(init,n)
    best=a.copy(); best_obj=_objective(a)
    N=_next_pow2(2*n-1)
    FA=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    log_a=np.log(a)
    g=a.copy(); log_g=np.log(g)
    tau=n*0.07
    tau_min=max(0.5,n*1e-4)
    eta=1.0; cap=0.8
    inner_base=6
    no_imp=0
    period=0
    while time<deadline:
       # adaptive direct step every 4
       if period % 4 == 0:
          b=_self_conv_from_fa(FA)
          p=_softmax(b,tau)
          d=_cross_from_fa(FA,p,n,N)
          inc=_update_increment(eta, d, cap)
          log_a=log_a+inc
          a,log_a=_normalize_log(log_a,n,MAX_LOG_RANGE)
          FA=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
       # inner minimax
       inner_iters = inner_base + int(8 * min(1, elapsed/budget))
       for _ in range(inner_iters):
          G=np.fft.rfft(g,N)
          c=np.fft.irfft(FA*G,N)[:2*n-1].real; c=np.clip(c,0)
          p=_softmax(c,tau)
          d=_cross_from_fa(FA,p,n,N)
          inc=_update_increment(eta,d,cap)
          log_g=log_g+inc
          g,log_g=_normalize_log(log_g,n,MAX_LOG_RANGE)
       # evaluate g: G currently? after inner G from g last; if inner_iters=0 no.
       G=np.fft.rfft(g,N)
       gmax=np.max(np.fft.irfft(G*G,N)[:2*n-1])
       og=2*gmax/n
       curmax=np.max(np.fft.irfft(FA*FA,N)[:2*n-1])
       aa=2*curmax/n
       # choose update a
       # Possibly evaluate candidate blends: lam 1,0.5,0.2
       new_a=a; new_obj=aa
       candidates=[]
       if og < aa:
           if og < aa*0.95: candidates=[(1.0,g,(1.0)*a+0*g), (0.6,...)]
           else: candidates=[(0.5,...)]
       else:
           candidates=[(0.1,...)]
       # include g if og
       for lam in [1.0,0.6,0.3] maybe limited 2:
          cand=(1-lam)*a+lam*g
          obj=_objective_fft? We can compute via FFT; but to reduce, use internal function _objective_from_seq(cand,N)
          if obj<new_obj: new_a=cand; new_obj=obj
       # update best
       if new_obj < best_obj*(1-1e-8): best=new_a; best_obj=new_obj; no_imp=0
       else: no_imp+=1
       a=new_a
       log_a=np.log(a) # could use normalize_log to enforce range
       a,log_a=_normalize_log(log_a,n,MAX_LOG_RANGE)
       FA=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
       # keep g close to a: if no_imp high reset g
       if no_imp > 80:
           a=best.copy()
           noise_scale=0.1+0.2*(no_imp/200)
           a=np.exp(rng.normal(0,noise_scale,n))*a
           a,log_a=_normalize_log(np.log(a),n,MAX_LOG_RANGE)
           g=a.copy(); log_g=log_a.copy(); FA=np.fft.rfft(a,N); no_imp=0
       tau*=0.99
       if tau<tau_min: tau=tau_min
       eta = min(50, eta*1.05) maybe if changes small? We can track max inc.
       period+=1
    return best,best_obj
```

Let's refine `_exp_refine` to be efficient and stable. We'll write helper functions for update increment:
```
def _make_inc(d, eta, cap):
    if d.size==0: return d
    vmax = d.max()
    inc = (vmax - d) * eta
    vmax_inc = inc.max()
    # adaptive? return inc capped
    return np.clip(inc, 0.0, cap)
```
Since nonnegative. If d all nan? ensure clip.
Adaptive eta: outside maintain `eta`; after computing inc before clip, if inc.max() < 0.01: eta=min(eta*1.5,50); elif inc.max()>cap*0.9: eta=max(eta*0.8,0.05). But inc.max depends. We can implement in loop.
But if eta increases during inner, okay.

Temperature adaptive: We can compute a quick entropy of p: `ent = -np.sum(p*np.log(p+1e-300))`. If ent < 0.5 (too concentrated), maybe increase tau: tau*=1.5. If ent > 0.5*np.log(2*n-1) (too uniform), decrease tau. But p length 2n; computing log p O(n) okay. Might help. Use simple:
```
if p.size>0:
    pp=p[p>0]
    ent=-np.sum(pp*np.log(pp))
    target_ent = np.log(2*n) * 0.2  # moderate
    if ent < target_ent*0.5: tau *= 1.2
    elif ent > np.log(2*n): tau *= 0.9
    tau = clip(tau, tau_min, tau_max)
```
But too much. Maybe skip.

Candidate evaluation in exp: To save FFT, we can compute objective for blends via internal function `_objective_with_N(cand,N)`; that's one FFT per cand. We can evaluate 2 candidates.
We need current aa objective. Compute `cur_b = _self_conv_fa(FA)` each outer. Use for direct and aa. After direct update FA changes; recompute.
Pseudo code more precise:
```
def _exp_refine(init,n,budget,seed=0):
    t0=time.monotonic(); deadline=t0+max(0,budget-2)
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    a=_normalize_seq(init,n)
    best=a.copy(); best_obj=_objective_with_n(a,n)
    N=_next_pow2(2*n-1)
    FA=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    log_a=np.log(a)
    g=a.copy(); log_g=np.log(g)
    tau=0.1*n
    tau_min=max(0.25, n*1e-4)
    tau_max=0.5*n
    eta=1.0; cap=1.0; range_cap=6.0
    no_imp=0; iter=0; last_eta_adj=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        # direct step every 3
        if iter % 3 == 0:
            b=_self_conv_fa(FA)
            p=_softmax(b,tau)
            d=_cross_fa(FA,p)
            inc0,eta = _update_eta(eta,_make_inc_raw(d,eta,cap),cap,0.02)
            d_inc=np.clip(inc_raw,0,cap)
            log_a=log_a+d_inc
            a,log_a=_normalize_log(log_a,n,range_cap)
            FA=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
            # maybe if a best
            obj=_objective_fa(FA)
            if obj<best_obj: best=a.copy(); best_obj=obj; no_imp=0
            else: no_imp+=1
        # inner
        inner_iters = 4 + int(8 * min(1.0, (time.monotonic()-t0)/max(1.0,budget)))
        for _ in range(inner_iters):
            G=np.fft.rfft(g,N)
            c=np.fft.irfft(FA*G,N)[:2*n-1].real; c=npmaximum(c,0)
            p=_softmax(c,tau)
            d=_cross_fa(FA,p)
            inc_raw,eta = _update_eta(eta,_make_inc_raw(d,eta,cap),cap,0.02)
            log_g=log_g+np.clip(inc_raw,0,cap)
            g,log_g=_normalize_log(log_g,n,range_cap)
        # evaluate g
        G=np.fft.rfft(g,N)
        og=_objective_fa_from(G) # compute max
        curb=_self_conv_fa(FA); aa=2*curb.max()/n
        # choose update
        new_a=a; new_obj=aa
        # Candidate g if og finite
        if np.isfinite(og):
           cand=g; obj=og
           if obj<new_obj: new_a=cand; new_obj=obj
           # blend depending
           lams=[0.5,0.2]
           if og < aa*0.98: lams=[0.7,1.0?]
           for lam in lams:
              cand=(1-lam)*a+lam*g
              cand=_normalize_seq(cand,n)
              obj=_objective_with_N(cand,N)
              if obj<new_obj: new_a=cand; new_obj=obj
        a,log_a,FA = _set_a(new_a,N) # normalize, log, fft
        if new_obj<best_obj*(1-1e-8): best=new_a.copy(); best_obj=new_obj; no_imp=0
        else: no_imp+=1
        # reset if stuck
        if no_imp > 60:
           a=best.copy()
           scale_noise = 0.1 + 0.1*min(1.0,no_imp/200.0)
           a = a * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,scale_noise,n))
           a,log_a=_normalize_log(np.log(a),n,range_cap)
           g=a.copy(); log_g=log_a.copy()
           FA=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
           no_imp=0
        tau *= 0.99
        if tau < tau_min: tau=tau_min
        if tau > tau_max: tau=tau_max
        iter +=1
    return best,best_obj
```
Need `_objective_fa(FA)` returns 2*max(irfft(FA*FA))/n. `_objective_fa_from(G)` similar but G from g. But if g sum not exactly n due to normalize_log yes. Use `_max_self_fft(F)`.
`_normalize_seq(cand,n)` clips and normalizes; it may alter log range? For objective okay. After choosing new_a, use `_normalize_log(np.log(new_a), n, range_cap)` to enforce and set log.
Potential performance: In direct step and inner, each iteration calls `_softmax` which loops O(n); okay.

def _scale_eta(eta, inc_max, cap, small=0.02):
   if inc_max < small: return min(eta*1.5, 30.0)
   if inc_max > cap*0.9: return max(eta*0.7, 0.05)
   return eta
```

Now LP: `_lp_refine` should also maybe use exponential if too slow. Let's implement robust.
```
def _lp_refine(init,n,budget,seed=0,lp_timeout=4):
   t0; deadline=...
   if not HAVE_SCIPY or budget<10: return _exp_refine(...)
   a=_normalize_seq(init,n)
   best=a.copy(); best_obj=_objective(a)
   fail=0
   last_solve=0
   while time<deadline:
      t_start=time.monotonic()
      a1=a/n
      # If n too large and previous solve slow, reduce? We'll just call with given n.
      c = np.zeros(2*n-1); c[:n]=a1
      r = np.zeros(2*n-1); r[0]=a1[0]
      try: T=toeplitz(c,r)
      except: T=... loop
      A_ub = np.hstack((T, -np.ones((2*n-1,1))))
      obj = np.zeros(n+1); obj[-1]=1
      A_eq = np.array([[np.ones(n),0.0]])
      b_eq = np.array([1.0])
      bounds = [(0.0,None)]*n + [(0.0,None)]
      res=linprog(obj, A_ub, np.zeros(2*n-1), A_eq, b_eq, bounds=bounds, method='highs', timeout=lp_timeout)
      if not res.success:
         fail+=1
         if fail>3: break
      else:
         fail=0
         g=np.clip(res.x[:n],0,None)
         s=g.sum()
         if s<1e-9: continue
         g = g*(n/s)
         # candidates
         new_a=a; new_obj=_objective(a)
         # evaluate g and blends
         for lam in (1.0,0.7,0.5,0.3,0.1):
            cand=(1-lam)*a+lam*g
            cand=_normalize_seq(cand,n)
            objv=_objective(cand)
            if objv<new_obj:
                new_a=cand; new_obj=objv
            if objv<best_obj:
                best=cand; best_obj=objv
         a=new_a
         # Maybe eta? no
      elapsed_solve=time.monotonic()-t_start
      # if solve very slow relative, break? if elapsed_solve > 20 and time-left <30: break
      if time.monotonic()-last_solve: ...
      if fail>=3: break
   return best,best_obj
```
`_objective` uses conv each candidate; for n=1024 and 5 candidates, okay.
But LP with A_ub dense hstack may memory spike. Use n<=1024. For n=1024, T 2047x1024 ~1.68M 13MB, A_ub ~14MB, okay. Solver may copy. Good.
Time per LP maybe several seconds. Set `lp_timeout=3` to allow many. But presolve maybe needs more; if timeout, fail. Could use 8. We'll set based on n: `lp_timeout = 2 if n<=512 else 5`.
Maybe HiGHS `timeout` option not supported? It is a parameter of linprog. If not, might raise? Check docs: `scipy.optimize.linprog` has `timeout` argument in SciPy >=1.6. Likely. To be safe, wrap with try; if TypeError, call without timeout.
```
try: res=linprog(..., timeout=lp_timeout)
except TypeError: res=linprog(...)
```
Options: `options={'presolve': 'default'}` maybe accepted. If not, omit. Use simple.

def _objective_with_N(a,N):
    n=a.size; S=a.sum(); if S<=1e-15: return np.inf
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N); b=irfft(fa*fa,N)[:2*n-1]; return float(2*n*b.max()/(S*S))
```

def _normalize_seq(seq, n, scale=n):
    a=np.asarray(seq,dtype=float).ravel()
    if a.size != n:
       if a.size==0: a=np.ones(n)
       else: a=np.interp(np.linspace(0,1,n), np.linspace(0,1,a.size), a)
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    s=a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-15:
       a=np.ones(n,dtype=float)
       s=n
    a = a * (scale / s)
    # Ensure max <= 1000? Since scale=n and range maybe; clip if max>1000 and renormalize? Clipping could change shape. But safe.
    if a.max() > 1000.0:
       a = np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
       s=a.sum()
       if s>0: a = a*(scale/s)
       # If still max >1000? cannot, sum scale maybe if one element 1000 and scale large, after renorm could exceed? Suppose scale=4096, one clip 1000, sum 1000, scale to 4 -> >1000. Loop? `_normalize_log` with range cap:
```
def _normalize_log(log_a, n, range_cap=MAX_LOG_RANGE, scale=None):
    if scale is None: scale=float(n)
    x=np.asarray(log_a,dtype=float)
    if x.size != n: ...
    x=np.where(np.isfinite(x),x,-range_cap)
    mx=x.max()
    if not np.isfinite(mx) or mx == -np.inf:
       a=np.ones(n); loga=np.zeros(n); return a,loga
    x=x-mx
    x=np.clip(x, -range_cap, 0.0)
    a=np.exp(x)
    s=a.sum()
    if s <= 0: ...
    scale_f=scale/s
    a*=scale_f
    loga=x+np.log(scale_f)
    return a, loga
```
If range cap 6 and scale n, safe.

Global `_COS_STATE`:
```
_COS_STATE = {'bases': None, 'n': 0, 'amp': 2.0, 'range': MAX_LOG_RANGE}
def _cos_objective_state(x):
    x=np.asarray(x,dtype=float)
    c=_COS_STATE['amp'] * np.tanh(np.clip(x, -20, 20))
    xseq=np.zeros(_COS_STATE['n'])
    bases=_COS_STATE['bases']
    for i in range(len(bases)):
        xseq += c[i]*bases[i]
    a, _ = _normalize_log(xseq, _COS_STATE['n'], _COS_STATE['range'])
    N=_next_pow2(2*_COS_STATE['n']-1)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    b=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N)[:2*_COS_STATE['n']-1].real
    return float(2*_COS_STATE['n']*b.max()/(a.sum()*a.sum()))
```
This recomputes N each eval; can store in state for speed.
Set `_COS_STATE['N']`.

Detailed implementation of `_add_top`:
```
def _add_top(top, seq, score, max_keep=10):
    if seq is None or not np.isfinite(score): return top
    arr=np.asarray(seq, dtype=float)
    if arr.size ==0: return top
    arr=arr.ravel()
    arr=np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr=np.clip(arr,0.0,1000.0)
    if arr.sum() <= 0: return top
    top.append((float(score), arr))
    if len(top)>max_keep:
        # find index with largest score
        worst=0
        for i in range(1,len(top)):
            if top[i][0] > top[worst][0]: worst=i
        top.pop(worst)
    return top
```
No sorting. At final evaluate all top.
Note: arr stored as np array; memory 10*4096 fine.

def _finalize_top(top):
    if not top: return np.ones(1024), 2.0
    best_seq=None; best_true=np.inf
    # If length > 8192, don't evaluate many? We'll limit evaluation by time? Use internal as score if too long.
    for score, arr in top:
       if len(arr) <= 6000: # evaluate true
          try: s=float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in arr]))
          except Exception: s=score
          if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_true:
             best_true=s; best_seq=arr
       else:
          # use internal objective to compare
          s=score
          if s < best_true: best_true=s; best_seq=arr
    if best_seq is None: best_seq=top[0][1]
    return _safe_list(best_seq), best_true
```

```

`_safe_list`:
```
def _safe_list(arr):
    a=np.asarray(arr,dtype=float).ravel()
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a),a,0.0)
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    if a.size==0: a=np.ones(256)
    s=a.sum()
    # Ensure sum >=0.01 after evaluator normals? Evaluator doesn't normalize; if sum very small returns inf. Our sequences sum n, safe. If clipped sum small, normalize to 1.
    if s < 0.01:
       a = a / s if s>0 else None; if None: ones
    return [float(x) for x in a]
```

Define `_objective_seq` generic with FFT:
```
def _objective_seq(seq):
   a=np.asarray(seq,dtype=float).ravel(); n=a.size; if n<1 return inf
   a=np.where(np.isfinite(a),a,0.0); a=np.clip(a,0,1000); S=a.sum(); if S<1e-8 return inf
   N=_next_pow2(2*n-1); fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N); b=irfft(fa*fa,N)[:2*n-1]; return float(2*n*b.max()/(S*S))
```
Use for hs and generic.

Now, exponential refine candidate objective: We can use `_objective_with_N` which assumes a length n and N. It computes sum; no range. We'll prepare candidates with normalize_log to sum n, then objective can be simply 2*max/n. But generic with sum safe.

Let's write `_exp_refine` carefully with time.
```
def _exp_refine(init_seq, n, budget_s, seed=0, range_cap=MAX_LOG_RANGE):
    t_start = time.monotonic()
    budget_s = max(1.0, float(budget_s))
    deadline = t_start + max(0.0, budget_s - 2.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    a = _prepare_seq_log(init_seq, n, range_cap)  # returns normalized age and log
    a, log_a = ...
    best_a = a.copy(); best_obj = _objective_with_N(a, N)
    N=...
    FA=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    g=a.copy(); log_g=log_a.copy()
    tau = max(1.0, 0.1*n)
    tau_min = max(0.5, 1e-4*n)
    tau_max = 0.5*n
    eta=1.0; cap=1.0
    no_imp=0; iter=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       # if time remaining < 1 break
       # direct step
       if iter % 4 == 0:
          b = np.fft.irfft(FA*FA,N, n=N).real[:2*n-1]; b=np.maximum(b,0)
          p = _softmax(b, tau)
          d = _cross_from_fa(FA,p,n,N)
          inc_raw = (d.max()-d)*eta
          eta = _adapt_eta(eta, inc_raw.max(), cap)
          inc = np.clip(inc_raw, 0.0, cap)
          log_a = log_a + inc
          a, log_a = _normalize_log(log_a, n, range_cap, float(n))
          FA = np.fft.rfft(a,N)
          obj = _objective_with_N(a,N)
          if obj < best_obj:
             best_a=a.copy(); best_obj=obj; no_imp=0
          else:
             no_imp += 1
       # inner
       inner_iters = 3 + int(5 * min(1.0, (time.monotonic()-t_start)/max(1.0,budget_s)))
       for _ in range(inner_iters):
          G = np.fft.rfft(g,N)
          c = np.fft.irfft(FA*G, N, n=N).real[:2*n-1]
          c = np.maximum(c,0)
          p = _softmax(c, tau)
          d = _cross_from_fa(FA,p,n,N)
          inc_raw = (d.max()-d)*eta
          eta = _adapt_eta(eta, inc_raw.max(), cap)
          log_g = log_g + np.clip(inc_raw, 0.0, cap)
          g, log_g = _normalize_log(log_g, n, range_cap, float(n))
       # evaluate g and blends
       G = np.fft.rfft(g,N)
       gb = np.fft.irfft(G*G,N,n=N).real[:2*n-1]
       og = float(2*n*gb.max()/(g.sum()*g.sum()))
       curFB = np.fft.irfft(FA*FA,N,n=N).real[:2*n-1]
       aa = float(2*n*curFB.max()/(a.sum()*a.sum()))
       new_a = a; new_obj = aa
       # candidate g
       if np.isfinite(og):
          if og < new_obj:
             new_a = g; new_obj = og
       # blend candidates depending
       # use a small set of lams to limit cost
       if og < aa:
          lams = (0.5, 0.75)
       else:
          lams = (0.25,)
       for lam in lams:
          cand = (1.0-lam)*a + lam*g
          cand, _ = _prepare_log(cand, n, range_cap)
          co = _objective_with_N(cand,N)
          if co < new_obj:
             new_a = cand; new_obj = co
       # set a
       a, log_a = _prepare_log(new_a, n, range_cap)
       FA = np.fft.rfft(a,N)
       if new_obj < best_obj * (1.0 - 1e-8):
          best_a = a.copy(); best_obj = new_obj; no_imp=0
       else:
          no_imp += 1
       # reset if stuck
       if no_imp > 80:
          a = best_a.copy()
          noise_scale = 0.1 + 0.15*min(1.0, no_imp/500.0)
          noise = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, noise_scale, n))
          a = a * noise
          a, log_a = _prepare_log(a, n, range_cap)
          g = a.copy(); log_g = log_a.copy()
          FA = np.fft.rfft(a,N)
          no_imp = 0
       tau *= 0.992
       if tau < tau_min: tau=tau_min
       if tau > tau_max: tau=tau_max
       iter += 1
       if iter % 200 == 0 and verbose? no print? We can print progress from top-level? Could print inside; okay. Use print sparingly: every 200 outer print best_obj. But maybe many lines. We'll print every 500.
    return best_a, best_obj
```
Need `_prepare_log(seq,n,range_cap)` returns a,log_a. It normalizes and log. Define:
```
def _prepare_log(seq,n,range_cap):
    arr=np.asarray(seq,dtype=float).ravel()
    if arr.size != n: ... interp
    arr=np.where(np.isfinite(arr),arr,0.0)
    arr=np.clip(arr,0.0,1000.0)
    if arr.max() <= 0:
       a=np.ones(n); loga=np.zeros(n); return a,loga
    # To avoid log(0), set small = 1e-300
    la=np.log(arr)+np.log(1? no) ; z=arr<1e-300; la[z]=-700
    return _normalize_log(la,n,range_cap,float(n))
```
But for candidates with zeros, log -inf; `_normalize_log` replaces nonfinite with -range_cap, so zeros become min positive. That's okay.

`_prepare_seq_log` initial if seq length not n: interpolate. Use `_upsample`? Define:
```
def _resize_seq(seq,n):
   arr=np.asarray(seq,dtype=float).ravel()
   if arr.size==0: return np.ones(n)
   if arr.size==n: return arr
   x0=np.linspace(0,1,arr.size); x1=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   return np.interp(x1,x0,arr)
```

```
def _adapt_eta(eta, inc_max, cap):
    if inc_max < 0.01:
       eta = min(eta*1.3, 30.0)
    elif inc_max > cap*0.9:
       eta = max(eta*0.8, 0.05)
    return eta
```

def _coeffs_to_log_cos_seq(coeffs, n, range_cap):
    t=np.linspace(0,1,n) if n>1 else np.array([0.0])
    x=np.zeros(n)
    for i,c in enumerate(coeffs):
       x += c*np.cos((2*i+1)*np.pi*t)
    a,_=_normalize_log(x,n,range_cap)
    return a

One more mathematical check: The update uses `d.max()-d`; if p distribution has high lag central, d maybe equals mirror a. Suppose a is edge high at i=0 and low at n-1. central mirror: d_0=a_{n-1} (low) => inc high, so increases low edge? Wait d_i high when mirror a high. At i=0, mirror n-1 low, d low, inc high => increases a_0 (already high edge). That seems wrong! Let's analyze. For central active k=n-1, b_k=∑ a_i a_{n-1-i}. Coordinate contribution derivative for a_i is a_{n-1-i}. If a_0 high and a_{n-1} low, d_0=low, so increasing a_0 would increase b by low amount; d_{n-1}=high, increasing a_{n-1} would increase b a lot. ```python
"""
Log-domain multiplicative-weights search for nonnegative sequences minimizing

    score(a) = 2*n*max(a*a) / (sum(a)**2)

The main ideas are:

1. Parametrize smooth one-edge/alternating shapes with odd cosine modes in log
   domain:  a_i = exp(sum_m c_m * cos((2m+1)*pi*i/(n-1))).
2. Optimize those low-dimensional coefficients with a Nelder-Mead polish plus
   local random perturbations. This gives strong nonuniform initializations.
3. Refine the full sequence in log space using FFT-based multiplicative-weights
   steps.  The step uses a soft distribution over the largest autocorrelation
   lags and increases coordinates that have small marginal contribution to those
   lags.  A small inner game/Hedge step also tries to find sequences with small
   cross-correlation to the current one, in the spirit of the known linear
   program iteration, but without dense LPs.
4. Run for several lengths and starting points, keep the best candidates, and
   evaluate the final top candidates with the provided evaluate_sequence()
   function when it is cheap enough.

The function propose_candidate() is the required entry point.
"""

import time
import numpy as np

try:
    from scipy.optimize import minimize as _scipy_minimize
    HAVE_SCIPY = True
except Exception:
    HAVE_SCIPY = False

MAX_LOG_RANGE = 6.0
_TOP_KEEP = 10


def _next_pow2(m):
    m = max(1, int(m))
    n = 1
    while n < m:
        n <<= 1
    return max(2, n)


def _resize_array(arr, n):
    arr = np.asarray(arr, dtype=float).ravel()
    if arr.size == 0:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=float)
    if arr.size == n:
        return arr
    if arr.size == 1:
        return np.full(n, float(arr[0]), dtype=float)
    x0 = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, arr.size)
    x1 = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, int(n))
    out = np.interp(x1, x0, arr)
    out = np.where(np.isfinite(out), out, 0.0)
    return out


def _safe_array(arr, n):
    a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=float).ravel()
    if a.size != int(n):
        a = _resize_array(a, int(n))
    else:
        a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return a


def _normalize_log(log_a, n, range_cap, scale=None):
    """Convert log-weights to a positive sequence with sum=scale and bounded
     log-range, and return the corresponding consistent log-weights."""
    n = int(n)
    if scale is None:
        scale = float(n)
    x = np.asarray(log_a, dtype=float).ravel()
    if x.size != n:
        x = _resize_array(x, n)
    x = np.where(np.isfinite(x), x, -range_cap)
    mx = x.max()
    if not np.isfinite(mx):
        a = np.ones(n, dtype=float)
        la = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
        return a, la
    x = x - mx
    x = np.clip(x, -range_cap, 0.0)
    a = np.exp(x)
    s = a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300:
        a = np.ones(n, dtype=float)
        la = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
        return a, la
    sc = float(scale) / float(s)
    a = a * sc
    la = x + np.log(sc)
    return a, la


def _prepare_log(seq, n, range_cap=MAX_LOG_RANGE):
    a = _safe_array(seq, n)
    if a.max() <= 0.0:
        a = np.ones(n, dtype=float)
        la = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
        return a, la
    la = np.full(n, -range_cap, dtype=float)
    pos = a > 0.0
    la[pos] = np.log(a[pos])
    a, la = _normalize_log(la, n, range_cap, float(n))
    return a, la


def _objective_seq(seq):
    a = np.asarray(seq, dtype=float).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    n = int(a.size)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-12:
        return np.inf
    N = _next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    b = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N).real[:2 * n - 1]
    b = np.maximum(b, 0.0)
    return float(2.0 * n * float(b.max()) / (s * s))


def _objective_with_N(a, N):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=float).ravel()
    n = int(a.size)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-12:
        return np.inf
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    b = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N).real[:2 * n - 1]
    b = np.maximum(b, 0.0)
    return float(2.0 * n * float(b.max()) / (s * s))


def _softmax_weights(b, tau):
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=float).ravel()
    if b.size == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=float)
    if tau <= 1e-12:
        w = np.zeros(b.size, dtype=float)
        w[int(np.argmax(b))] = 1.0
        return w
    m = float(b.max())
    x = (b - m) / float(tau)
    x = np.clip(x, -745.0, 0.0)
    w = np.exp(x)
    s = float(w.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        w = np.zeros(b.size, dtype=float)
        w[int(np.argmax(b))] = 1.0
        return w
    return w / s


def _cross_from_fa(FA, p, n, N):
    """d_i = sum_k p_k * a_{k-i}, given FFta FA of a and lag weights p."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=float).ravel()
    fp = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    corr = np.fft.irfft(FA * np.conj(fp), N).real
    d = np.empty(int(n), dtype=float)
    d[0] = corr[0]
    if n > 1:
        idx = N - np.arange(1, int(n), dtype=int)
        d[1:] = corr[idx]
    d = np.where(np.isfinite(d), d, 0.0)
    return np.maximum(d, 0.0)


def _adapt_eta(eta, inc_max, cap):
    if not np.isfinite(inc_max):
        return 1.0
    if inc_max < 0.01:
        eta = min(eta * 1.3, 30.0)
    elif inc_max > 0.9 * cap:
        eta = max(eta * 0.8, 0.05)
    return eta


def _make_log_cos_seq(coeffs, n, range_cap=MAX_LOG_RANGE):
    n = int(n)
    coeffs = np.asarray(coeffs, dtype=float).ravel()
    if n == 1:
        a = np.array([float(np.exp(float(coeffs.sum())))], dtype=float)
        a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
        if a.sum() > 0:
            a *= float(n) / a.sum()
        else:
            a = np.array([1.0], dtype=float)
        return a
    t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    x = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    pi = np.pi
    for i, c in enumerate(coeffs):
        if abs(c) > 0.0:
            x += float(c) * np.cos((2 * i + 1) * pi * t)
    a, _ = _normalize_log(x, n, range_cap, float(n))
    return a


def _coeffs_to_log_cos_seq_from_bases(coeffs, bases, n, range_cap=MAX_LOG_RANGE):
    x = np.zeros(int(n), dtype=float)
    for i, c in enumerate(coeffs):
        if abs(c) > 0.0 and i < len(bases):
            x += float(c) * bases[i]
    a, _ = _normalize_log(x, int(n), range_cap, float(n))
    return a


def _cos_bases(n, K):
    n = int(n)
    K = int(K)
    if n == 1:
        return [np.zeros(1)] * K
    t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    pi = np.pi
    return [np.cos((2 * i + 1) * pi * t) for i in range(K)]


_COS_STATE = {
    "n": 0,
    "K": 0,
    "amp": 1.0,
    "range": MAX_LOG_RANGE,
    "bases": [],
    "N": 2,
}


def _cos_objective_state(x):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=float).ravel()
    amp = float(_COS_STATE["amp"])
    c = amp * np.tanh(np.clip(x, -20.0, 20.0))
    n = int(_COS_STATE["n"])
    xseq = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    bases = _COS_STATE["bases"]
    for i in range(len(bases)):
        if abs(c[i]) > 0.0:
            xseq += float(c[i]) * bases[i]
    a, _ = _normalize_log(xseq, n, float(_COS_STATE["range"]), float(n))
    N = int(_COS_STATE["N"])
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    b = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N).real[:2 * n - 1]
    b = np.maximum(b, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-12:
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * float(b.max()) / (s * s))


def _optimize_cos_set(n, K, budget_s, init_coeffs, seed=0, amp=1.2,
                      range_cap=MAX_LOG_RANGE):
    """Optimize odd-cosine log coefficients for a fixed length n."""
    n = int(n)
    K = max(1, int(min(K, max(1, n // 64)) if n >= 256 else 1))
    K = int(K)
    budget_s = max(1.0, float(budget_s))
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    t_start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t_start + max(0.0, budget_s - 1.0)

    coeffs0 = np.zeros(K, dtype=float)
    if init_coeffs is not None:
        ic = np.asarray(init_coeffs, dtype=float).ravel()
        L = min(K, ic.size)
        if L > 0:
            coeffs0[:L] = ic[:L]

    bases = _cos_bases(n, K)
    N = _next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    _COS_STATE["n"] = n
    _COS_STATE["K"] = K
    _COS_STATE["amp"] = float(amp)
    _COS_STATE["range"] = float(range_cap)
    _COS_STATE["bases"] = bases
    _COS_STATE["N"] = N

    # Convert coefficient guesses to unconstrained tanh variables.
    z0 = np.zeros(K, dtype=float)
    safe = np.clip(coeffs0 / float(amp), -0.98, 0.98)
    z0 = np.arctanh(safe)

    try:
        f0 = _cos_objective_state(z0)
    except Exception:
        f0 = np.inf

    if not np.isfinite(f0):
        z_best = z0
        f_best = np.inf
    else:
        z_best = z0
        f_best = f0

        if HAVE_SCIPY and time.monotonic() < deadline - 5.0:
            try:
                t_eval0 = time.monotonic()
                _ = _cos_objective_state(z0)
                t_eval = max(1e-5, time.monotonic() - t_eval0)
                maxiter = int(np.clip(30.0 / max(1.0, t_eval) * 0.35 * budget_s, 30, 900))
                maxiter = int(min(maxiter, 900))
                res = _scipy_minimize(
                    _cos_objective_state,
                    z_best,
                    method="Nelder-Mead",
                    options={
                        "maxiter": maxiter,
                        "xatol": 1e-6,
                        "fatol": 1e-9,
                        "adaptive": True,
                    },
                )
                if res is not None and np.all(np.isfinite(res.x)) and np.isfinite(res.fun):
                    if res.fun < f_best:
                        z_best = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=float)
                        f_best = float(res.fun)
            except Exception:
                pass

    # Short random-polish stage, kept inside the remaining budget.
    if np.isfinite(f_best) and time.monotonic() < deadline:
        step = 0.25
        while time.monotonic() < deadline:
            z_try = z_best + rng.normal(0.0, step, K)
            try:
                f_try = _cos_objective_state(z_try)
            except Exception:
                f_try = np.inf
            if np.isfinite(f_try) and f_try < f_best * (1.0 - 1e-9):
                z_best = z_try
                f_best = float(f_try)
                step = min(1.0, step * 1.15)
            else:
                step *= 0.85
            if step < 0.015:
                break

    coeffs = float(amp) * np.tanh(z_best)
    a = _coeffs_to_log_cos_seq_from_bases(coeffs, bases, n, range_cap)
    if not np.isfinite(f_best):
        f_best = _objective_seq(a)
    return coeffs, float(f_best), a


def _exp_refine(init_seq, n, budget_s, seed=0, range_cap=MAX_LOG_RANGE, verbose=False):
    """Full log-space multiplicative/Hedge refinement."""
    n = int(n)
    budget_s = max(1.0, float(budget_s))
    t_start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t_start + max(0.0, budget_s - 2.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))

    a, log_a = _prepare_log(init_seq, n, range_cap)
    N = _next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_obj = _objective_with_N(a, N)
    FA = np.fft.rfft(a, N)

    g = a.copy()
    log_g = log_a.copy()

    tau_max = max(1.0, 0.5 * n)
    tau_min = max(0.25, 1e-4 * n)
    tau = min(tau_max, max(1.0, 0.10 * n))
    eta = 1.0
    cap = 1.0
    no_imp = 0
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        # Direct step on a itself.
        if it % 4 == 0:
            b = np.fft.irfft(FA * FA, N).real[:2 * n - 1]
            b = np.maximum(b, 0.0)
            p = _softmax_weights(b, tau)
            d = _cross_from_fa(FA, p, n, N)
            raw = (float(d.max()) - d) * eta
            eta = _adapt_eta(eta, float(raw.max()), cap)
            inc = np.clip(raw, 0.0, cap)
            log_a = log_a + inc
            a, log_a = _normalize_log(log_a, n, range_cap, float(n))
            FA = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
            obj = _objective_with_N(a, N)
            if obj < best_obj:
                best_a = a.copy()
                best_obj = obj
                no_imp = 0
            else:
                no_imp += 1

        # Inner hedge/minimax-style update for a cross-correlation direction g.
        inner_iters = 3 + int(5 * min(1.0, (time.monotonic() - t_start) / max(1.0, budget_s)))
        for _ in range(inner_iters):
            G = np.fft.rfft(g, N)
            c = np.fft.irfft(FA * G, N).real[:2 * n - 1]
            c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
            p = _softmax_weights(c, tau)
            d = _cross_from_fa(FA, p, n, N)
            raw = (float(d.max()) - d) * eta
            eta = _adapt_eta(eta, float(raw.max()), cap)
            inc = np.clip(raw, 0.0, cap)
            log_g = log_g + inc
            g, log_g = _normalize_log(log_g, n, range_cap, float(n))

        # Evaluate the current a, g, and a few blends.
        G = np.fft.rfft(g, N)
        gb = np.fft.irfft(G * G, N).real[:2 * n - 1]
        gb = np.maximum(gb, 0.0)
        sg = float(g.sum())
        og = float(2.0 * n * float(gb.max()) / (sg * sg)) if sg > 0 else np.inf

        cb = np.fft.irfft(FA * FA, N).real[:2 * n - 1]
        cb = np.maximum(cb, 0.0)
        sa = float(a.sum())
        aa_obj = float(2.0 * n * float(cb.max()) / (sa * sa)) if sa > 0 else np.inf

        new_a = a
        new_obj = aa_obj

        if np.isfinite(og) and og < new_obj:
            new_a = g
            new_obj = og

        if np.isfinite(og) and og < aa_obj:
            lams = (0.5, 0.75)
        else:
            lams = (0.25,)

        for lam in lams:
            cand = (1.0 - lam) * a + lam * g
            cand, _ = _prepare_log(cand, n, range_cap)
            oc = _objective_with_N(cand, N)
            if oc < new_obj:
                new_a = cand
                new_obj = oc

        a, log_a = _prepare_log(new_a, n, range_cap)
        FA = np.fft.rfft(a, N)

        if new_obj < best_obj * (1.0 - 1e-8):
            best_a = a.copy()
            best_obj = new_obj
            no_imp = 0
        else:
            no_imp += 1

        if no_imp > 80:
            a = best_a.copy()
            noise_scale = 0.10 + 0.15 * min(1.0, float(no_imp) / 500.0)
            a = a * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, noise_scale, n))
            a, log_a = _prepare_log(a, n, range_cap)
            g = a.copy()
            log_g = log_a.copy()
            FA = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
            no_imp = 0

        # Occasionally smooth the active-set weights if stuck.
        if no_imp > 20:
            tau = min(tau_max, tau * 1.08)
        else:
            tau *= 0.992
            if tau < tau_min:
                tau = tau_min
            if tau > tau_max:
                tau = tau_max

        it += 1
        if verbose and it % 500 == 0:
            print("exp_refine n=%d iter=%d obj=%.6f tau=%.3f eta=%.3f" %
                  (n, it, best_obj, tau, eta))

    return best_a, float(best_obj)


def _add_top(top, seq, score, max_keep=_TOP_KEEP):
    if seq is None:
        return top
    score = float(score)
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        return top
    arr = _safe_array(seq, seq.size if hasattr(seq, "size") else len(seq))
    if arr.size == 0 or arr.sum() <= 0.0:
        return top
    top.append((score, arr))
    if len(top) > int(max_keep):
        worst = 0
        for i in range(1, len(top)):
            if top[i][0] > top[worst][0]:
                worst = i
        top.pop(worst)
    return top


def _pick_best_top(top):
    best_score = np.inf
    best_arr = None
    for score, arr in top:
        if np.isfinite(score) and score < best_score:
            best_score = score
            best_arr = arr
    return best_score, best_arr


def _sort_indices_by_score(top):
    remaining = list(range(len(top)))
    order = []
    while remaining:
        minpos = 0
        for j in range(1, len(remaining)):
            if top[remaining[j]][0] < top[remaining[minpos]][0]:
                minpos = j
        order.append(remaining.pop(minpos))
    return order


def _safe_list(arr):
    a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=float).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        a = np.ones(512, dtype=float)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 0.01:
        if s > 0.0:
            a = a * (0.1 / s)
        else:
            a = np.ones(max(a.size, 256), dtype=float)
    return [float(x) for x in a]


def _finalize_top(top, max_eval_seconds=10.0):
    if not top:
        return _safe_list(np.ones(1024)), 2.0
    order = _sort_indices_by_score(top)
    best_score = np.inf
    best_arr = None
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    eval_count = 0

    for idx in order:
        internal_score, arr = top[idx]
        L = int(arr.size)
        tried_true = False
        if L <= 8192 and eval_count < 6 and (time.monotonic() - t0) < max_eval_seconds:
            try:
                s = float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in arr]))
                tried_true = True
            except Exception:
                s = internal_score
            eval_count += 1
        else:
            s = internal_score
        if not np.isfinite(s):
            s = internal_score

        if s < best_score:
            best_score = s
            best_arr = arr

        # If the internal score is much better than the best true score so far,
        # evaluate a few more promising long candidates if time allows.
        if (not tried_true and internal_score < best_score - 1e-6 and
                time.monotonic() - t0 < max_eval_seconds and eval_count < 8 and L <= 8192):
            try:
                s2 = float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in arr]))
                if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < best_score:
                    best_score = s2
                    best_arr = arr
                eval_count += 1
            except Exception:
                pass

    if best_arr is None:
        best_arr = top[order[0]][1]
    return _safe_list(best_arr), float(best_score)


def _decay_coeffs(K, base=0.7, rng=None):
    amps = np.array([1.0, 0.6, 0.4, 0.25, 0.15, 0.1, 0.06, 0.04, 0.02, 0.01], dtype=float)
    K = int(min(int(K), amps.size))
    if K <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=float)
    out = base * amps[:K]
    if rng is not None:
        signs = rng.choice([-1.0, 1.0], size=K)
        signs[0] = 1.0
        out = out * signs
    return out


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    """Search and return the best nonnegative sequence found."""
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    budget_s = max(30.0, float(budget_s))
    deadline = t0 + max(1.0, budget_s - 12.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    top = []

    # Candidate from any previously supplied construction.
    try:
        hs_raw = height_sequence_1
        hs = np.asarray(hs_raw, dtype=float).ravel()
        if hs.size > 0:
            hs = np.where(np.isfinite(hs), hs, 0.0)
            hs = np.clip(hs, 0.0, 1000.0)
            if hs.sum() > 0.0:
                hs_score = _objective_seq(hs)
                top = _add_top(top, hs, hs_score)
                if np.isfinite(hs_score):
                    print("initial height_sequence_1 score=%.6f len=%d" % (hs_score, hs.size))
    except Exception:
        pass

    # Choose main/large lengths based on available time.
    if budget_s >= 1000.0:
        n_main = 4096
        n_large = 8192
    elif budget_s >= 450.0:
        n_main = 2048
        n_large = 4096
    else:
        n_main = 1024
        n_large = 0

    def _time_left():
        return max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic())

    # Low-dimensional cosine shape optimization.
    if _time_left() > 120.0:
        c_budget1 = min(75.0, max(20.0, _time_left() * 0.08))
        if c_budget1 > 15.0:
            try:
                K1 = min(4, max(2, n_main // 64))
                init1 = _decay_coeffs(K1, base=0.85, rng=None)
                c, sc, seq = _optimize_cos_set(
                    n_main, K1, c_budget1, init1, seed=101, amp=1.6,
                    range_cap=MAX_LOG_RANGE
                )
                if np.isfinite(sc):
                    top = _add_top(top, seq, sc)
                    print("cos K=%d score=%.6f" % (K1, sc))
            except Exception as e:
                print("cos opt 1 failed:", e)

        if _time_left() > 120.0:
            c_budget2 = min(75.0, max(20.0, _time_left() * 0.08))
            if c_budget2 > 15.0:
                try:
                    K2 = min(8, max(4, n_main // 64))
                    init2 = _decay_coeffs(K2, base=0.75, rng=rng)
                    # Make the first coefficient reliably positive for edge contrast.
                    init2[0] = abs(init2[0]) + 0.35
                    c, sc, seq = _optimize_cos_set(
                        n_main, K2, c_budget2, init2, seed=202, amp=1.0,
                        range_cap=MAIN_LOG_RANGE
                    )
                    if np.isfinite(sc):
                        top = _add_top(top, seq, sc)
                        print("cos K=%d score=%.6f" % (K2, sc))
                except Exception as e:
                    print("cos opt 2 failed:", e)

    # If nothing good was found, create a few deterministic/random cosine starts.
    if not top or _pick_best_top(top)[0] > 1.999:
        starts = []
        starts.append(_make_log_cos_seq(_decay_coeffs(min(4, n_main // 64), base=0.85, rng=None), n_main))
        starts.append(_make_log_cos_seq(_decay_coeffs(min(8, n_main // 64), base=0.7, rng=rng), n_main))
        starts.append(_make_log_cos_seq(_decay_coeffs(min(6, n_main // 64), base=1.0, rng=rng), n_main))
        for s in starts:
            sc = _objective_seq(s)
            top = _add_top(top, s, sc)
        best_s, best_arr = _pick_best_top(top)
        print("prototype cosine best score=%.6f" % best_s)

    best_score, best_arr = _pick_best_top(top)
    if best_arr is None:
        best_arr = _make_log_cos_seq(_decay_coeffs(min(4, n_main // 64), base=0.9, rng=None), n_main)
        best_score = _objective_seq(best_arr)

    # Main full-sequence refinement.
    time_left = _time_left()
    main_budget = min(900.0, max(20.0, time_left * 0.55))
    if main_budget > 20.0:
        try:
            seq, sc = _exp_refine(best_arr, n_main, main_budget, seed=int(seed) + 7,
                                  range_cap=MAX_LOG_RANGE, verbose=True)
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                top = _add_top(top, seq, sc)
                print("main exp refine score=%.6f" % sc)
        except Exception as e:
            print("main exp refine failed:", e)

    # Alternate starts with the full refiner.
    time_left = _time_left()
    for alt_i in range(2):
        time_left = _time_left()
        if time_left < 120.0:
            break
        alt_budget = min(260.0, 0.25 * time_left)
        if alt_budget < 40.0:
            break
        try:
            if alt_i == 0:
                init_alt = _make_log_cos_seq(
                    _decay_coeffs(min(8, n_main // 64), base=0.8, rng=rng), n_main
                )
            else:
                # Random direction + strong edge term.
                K = min(8, max(4, n_main // 64))
                coeff = _decay_coeffs(K, base=0.7, rng=rng)
                coeff[0] = abs(coeff[0]) + 0.45
                init_alt = _make_log_cos_seq(coeff, n_main)
            seq, sc = _exp_refine(init_alt, n_main, alt_budget, seed=int(seed) + 100 + alt_i,
                                  range_cap=MAX_LOG_RANGE, verbose=False)
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                top = _add_top(top, seq, sc)
                print("alt exp refine %d score=%.6f" % (alt_i, sc))
        except Exception as e:
            print("alt exp refine failed:", e)

    # Larger length from the current best, if time remains.
    if n_large > 0:
        time_left = _time_left()
        if time_left > 260.0:
            best_score, best_arr = _pick_best_top(top)
            if best_arr is not None:
                big_budget = min(700.0, max(60.0, 0.55 * time_left))
                try:
                    init_big = _resize_array(best_arr, n_large)
                    seq, sc = _exp_refine(init_big, n_large, big_budget,
                                          seed=int(seed) + 900, range_cap=MAX_LOG_RANGE,
                                          verbose=True)
                    if np.isfinite(sc):
                        top = _add_top(top, seq, sc)
                        print("large exp refine n=%d score=%.6f" % (n_large, sc))
                except Exception as e:
                    print("large exp refine failed:", e)

    # If still above the uniform value, try one direct cosine sequence of a
    # different length as a safety net.
    if len(top) == 0 or _pick_best_top(top)[0] >= 2.0:
        n_fallback = min(4096, max(1024, n_main))
        try:
            coeffs = _decay_coeffs(min(8, n_fallback // 64), base=0.8, rng=rng)
            coeffs[0] = abs(coeffs[0]) + 0.5
            seq = _make_log_cos_seq(coeffs, n_fallback)
            seq, sc = _exp_refine(seq, n_fallback, max(30.0, _time_left() * 0.5),
                                  seed=int(seed) + 1234, range_cap=MAX_LOG_RANGE,
                                  verbose=False)
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                top = _add_top(top, seq, sc)
        except Exception:
            pass

    best_seq, best_score = _finalize_top(top, max_eval_seconds=8.0)
    if best_seq is None or len(best_seq) == 0:
        best_seq = _safe_list(_make_log_cos_seq(_decay_coeffs(4, base=0.9, rng=None), 2048))
        try:
            best_score = float(evaluate_sequence(best_seq))
        except Exception:
            best_score = 2.0

    print("final best score=%.6f len=%d" % (best_score, len(best_seq)))
    return best_seq
```
